## WINTER 1901 ADJUSTMENTS (`W1901A`)

<img src="W1901A.svg" alt="W1901A map" width="700">

### What happened

- **AUSTRIA**: builds F TRI; builds A VIE
- **ENGLAND**: builds F EDI
- **FRANCE**: builds A PAR; builds F BRE
- **GERMANY**: builds F KIE; builds A MUN
- **ITALY**: builds F NAP
- **RUSSIA**: builds F SEV; builds A WAR
- **TURKEY**: builds F SMY

### AUSTRIA
Orders: `F TRI B` · `A VIE B`

<details><summary>Reasoning</summary>

> I have 5 centers and 3 units, so I'm owed 2 builds. My open home centers are TRI and VIE. I want to keep flexibility for both a southern push against Turkey and pressure options. Building a fleet at TRI gives me Adriatic/Ionian reach to support the Lepanto and project naval power; building an army at VIE keeps me flexible to reinforce the Balkans or guard Galicia.
>
> A fleet at TRI is valuable for backing the eastern push (and supporting Greece/Aegean), while an army at VIE keeps me ready to move into the Balkans or hold against Russia.

</details>

### ENGLAND
Orders: `F EDI B`

<details><summary>Reasoning</summary>

> I have 4 centers and 3 units, so I'm owed one build. My plan calls for building toward the North Sea/Scandinavia to back Russia or squeeze Germany in the Baltic next year. A fleet at Edinburgh pushes north toward Skagerrak/Norway and keeps me flexible for the Baltic squeeze. I'll keep LVP/LON open for future army builds if needed, but a fleet is the right tool for my northern naval strategy.

</details>

### FRANCE
Orders: `A PAR B` · `F BRE B`

<details><summary>Reasoning</summary>

> I have 5 centers and 3 units, so I'm owed 2 builds. My plan for 1902 is to take Belgium from England's flank, which needs an army that can reach Belgium (via Burgundy or Picardy). I also want to keep fleet options open for the western Med and Atlantic. Building one army in Paris (to march toward Belgium) and one fleet in Brest (to support westward/Channel flexibility and Atlantic operations) gives me both a path to Belgium and naval strength. Brest fleet keeps me ready to contest the Channel if England's promises slip, while honoring the DMZ for now.

</details>

### GERMANY
Orders: `F KIE B` · `A MUN B`

<details><summary>Reasoning</summary>

> I'm at 5 centers, owed 2 builds. My plan calls for a fleet in Kiel for the Baltic and an army in Munich for flexibility. A Kiel fleet sets up the Russian Baltic squeeze with England in 1902; a Munich army keeps the western/southern option open. Both home centers are vacant, so both builds are legal.

</details>

### ITALY
Orders: `F NAP B`

<details><summary>Reasoning</summary>

> I have 4 centers and 3 units, so one build. My plan is the Lepanto against Turkey, which requires a fleet to convoy and support an eastward push. A fleet at Naples backs the eastern Mediterranean operation and pairs with F ION.

</details>

### RUSSIA
Orders: `F SEV B` · `A WAR B`

<details><summary>Reasoning</summary>

> I have 6 centers and 4 units, so I'm owed 2 builds. My open home centers are MOS, SEV, and WAR (STP is occupied). I want to position for pressing both the Baltic/Scandinavian front and the Black Sea/southern front in 1902.
>
> My strategy calls for fleets to press the Baltic and Black Sea. Turkey is building F CON as a "defensive" southern presence — I should hedge there. A fleet in Sevastopol backs up Rumania and lets me contest the Black Sea if Turkey turns. For the second build, an army in Warsaw keeps me flexible toward Galicia (pressure on Austria, per Turkey coordination talks) or defensive against Germany. This balances both fronts and keeps me growing aggressively.

</details>

### TURKEY
Orders: `F SMY B`

<details><summary>Reasoning</summary>

> I have 4 centers and 3 units, so I'm owed one build. My only open home center is SMY. I told both Russia and Austria I'd build a fleet in CON, but CON is now occupied by my A CON, so I must build in SMY instead.
>
> A fleet in SMY gives me southern naval presence and the option to enter the Aegean or eastern Med, but it can't reach the Black Sea quickly. Given Russia's growing fleet stack and my future plan to potentially contest Rumania or break the DMZ, an army is more flexible for the Balkan/Armenian theater — but a fleet better counters Russia's naval dominance and Italy's Ionian presence. Since my eastern ambitions hinge on naval reach (Black Sea via straits, or Aegean), and my land is already covered by A BUL and A CON, I'll build a fleet to project power south and set up flexibility against both Russia and Italy.

</details>

### ENGLAND

>

### GERMANY

<details><summary>Reasoning</summary>

Given France holds Munich-adjacent ground and I just reclaimed MUN, an army gives me flexibility to push inland or defend; but I already have A MUN and fleets covering the coast. I'd rather build a fleet to keep options for swinging into the Baltic or North Sea where England is overextended and France wants me to strike. A fleet in Berlin can reach BAL/PRU/KIE — good for a Baltic play.

</details>

### RUSSIA
Orders: `F BOT D`

<details><summary>Reasoning</summary>

A WAR holds my open home center for builds and connects to my eastern fronts, A STP/A FIN wall the corner, and F SEV defends against Turkey. </details>

### TURKEY

>

A fleet at SMY gives me more naval flexibility in the Aegean/Mediterranean for the southern push, but I already have ample fleet presence in the south (F AEG, F RUM). What I need for the Serbia line and to actually take Balkan land centers is an army. Building an army at ANK lets me march toward the southern front to support the Serbia push alongside Italy, or reinforce Bulgaria/Rumania. I'll build A ANK.

</details>

## FALL 1906 RETREATS (`F1906R`)

<img src="F1906R.svg" alt="F1906R map" width="700">

### What happened

- **AUSTRIA**: F TRI retreats → VEN
- **GERMANY**: F DEN retreats → BAL

### AUSTRIA
Orders: `F TRI R VEN`

<details><summary>Reasoning</summary>

> F TRI was dislodged by Italy. My only retreat option is VEN, which would push a unit into Italian home territory — likely to be dislodged or bounced next turn, but it keeps the unit alive and on the board rather than disbanding outright. With Italy's stack in the Adriatic, VEN is exposed but a live unit is better than none, and I lose no center either way. Retreating to VEN at least keeps a fleet that could harass Italy or be a bargaining chip.

</details>

### GERMANY
Orders: `F DEN R BAL`

<details><summary>Reasoning</summary>

> My F DEN was dislodged by England's coordinated attack (F SKA→DEN with multiple supports). England broke our quiet-coast deal and took Denmark — that changes everything. I need to retreat to save the unit. BAL keeps it active in the Baltic where it can defend Berlin/Kiel and contest the Baltic; HEL would just box it into the corner. BAL is the better square — it keeps the fleet relevant against England's now-hostile northern stack and protects my home centers.

</details>
